**Fix.** Add a user-space regression test that mounts `tmpfs`, checks `statfs()` and `fstatfs()` return `TMPFS_MAGIC`, and exercises a path below the mount so the `Path::fs()` behavior is covered.

### `kernel/src/fs/fs_impls/tmpfs/fs.rs` line 57

> ```diff
>         sb.magic = TMPFS_MAGIC;
>         sb.blocks = max_blocks;
>         sb.bfree = max_blocks;
>         sb.bavail = max_blocks;
>         sb.files = max_inodes;
>         sb.ffree = max_inodes;
> ```

Incorrect resource accounting (major): `TmpFs::sb()` reports `sb.bfree`, `sb.bavail`, and `sb.ffree` as the full limits on every call. A concrete failure is `mount -t tmpfs tmpfs /mnt`, write one `PAGE_SIZE` file, then call `statfs("/mnt")`: `f_bfree` still equals `f_blocks`, and creating files/directories likewise leaves `f_ffree` equal to `f_files`. If tmpfs limits/accounting are not implemented yet, do not advertise free capacity that never decreases.

# Summary

The change correctly moves `statfs`/`fstatfs` toward mount-level filesystem reporting, which is the right shape for tmpfs mounted on top of ramfs-backed inodes. The main remaining issue is correctness: tmpfs now advertises total/free block and inode capacity, but the free counts never decrease as files and directories consume resources. That can mislead userspace filesystem probes.

The next priority is coverage and maintainability around the workaround: add a regression test for tmpfs `statfs`/`fstatfs`, document the new compatibility behavior, and clean up the raw capacity policy/comment style so the workaround is easier to retire later.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/fs/fs_impls/tmpfs/fs.rs` line 48

> ```diff
> +        // TODO: Tmpfs currently wraps RamFs, whose inodes still point to the
> +        // inner RamFs. Override statfs-visible fields here as a workaround
> +        // rather than changing the semantics of ordinary RamFs instances.
> ```

`backtick-identifiers` (nit): The changed comment names Rust/API items as prose: `TmpFs` is written as `Tmpfs`, `RamFs` is not formatted as code, and `statfs` is an API name. That makes the workaround harder to connect to the actual types and syscall-facing behavior.

**Fix.** Format the identifiers and use the actual type spelling, for example:

```rust
// TODO: `TmpFs` currently wraps `RamFs`, whose inodes still point to the
// inner `RamFs`. Override `statfs`-visible fields here as a workaround
// rather than changing the semantics of ordinary `RamFs` instances.
```

### `kernel/src/fs/fs_impls/tmpfs/fs.rs` line 70

> ```diff
> +fn default_max_blocks() -> usize {
> +    crate::vm::mem_total() / PAGE_SIZE / 2
> +}
> +
> +fn default_max_inodes() -> usize {
> +    crate::vm::mem_total() / PAGE_SIZE / 2
> +}
> ```

`no-magic-number` (minor): The repeated `/ 2` encodes the default `TmpFs` capacity policy for both blocks and inodes, but the literal does not say why half of memory is the chosen limit or whether both helpers must stay coupled.

**Fix.** Give the policy a name and derive both helpers from it, or introduce one shared helper if the limits are intentionally identical, for example:

```rust
const DEFAULT_MAX_PAGES_DENOMINATOR: usize = 2;

fn default_max_pages() -> usize {
    crate::vm::mem_total() / PAGE_SIZE / DEFAULT_MAX_PAGES_DENOMINATOR
}
```

### `kernel/src/fs/vfs/path/mod.rs` line 103

> ```diff
> +    // Gets the file system of current `Path`.
> +    pub fn fs(&self) -> &Arc<dyn FileSystem> {
> +        self.mount.fs()
> +    }
> ```

`consistency` (nit): `Path::fs` is a public accessor placed among other documented `Path` accessors, but its comment uses plain `//`, so it is omitted from generated rustdoc while the neighboring `mount_node`, `dentry`, and `is_mount_root` methods use `///`.

**Fix.** Make this a rustdoc summary like the surrounding accessors:

```rust
/// Returns the file system of the current `Path`.
pub fn fs(&self) -> &Arc<dyn FileSystem> {
    self.mount.fs()
}
```

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/fs/fs_impls/tmpfs/fs.rs` line 55

> ```diff
>         sb.magic = TMPFS_MAGIC;
> ```

`add-regression-tests` (minor): This commit fixes user-visible `statfs`/`fstatfs` behavior for tmpfs, but it adds no regression test. A future change could reintroduce `RAMFS_MAGIC` for tmpfs paths or return the inode filesystem instead of the mount filesystem without any test failing.

**Fix.** Add a user-space regression test that mounts `tmpfs`, checks `statfs()` and `fstatfs()` return `TMPFS_MAGIC`, and exercises a path below the mount so the `Path::fs()` behavior is covered.

### `kernel/src/fs/fs_impls/tmpfs/fs.rs` line 57

> ```diff
>         sb.magic = TMPFS_MAGIC;
>         sb.blocks = max_blocks;
>         sb.bfree = max_blocks;
>         sb.bavail = max_blocks;
>         sb.files = max_inodes;
>         sb.ffree = max_inodes;
> ```

Incorrect resource accounting (major): `TmpFs::sb()` reports `sb.bfree`, `sb.bavail`, and `sb.ffree` as the full limits on every call. A concrete failure is `mount -t tmpfs tmpfs /mnt`, write one `PAGE_SIZE` file, then call `statfs("/mnt")`: `f_bfree` still equals `f_blocks`, and creating files/directories likewise leaves `f_ffree` equal to `f_files`. Userspace capacity probes therefore see stale free-space data after tmpfs usage changes.

**Fix.** Track aggregate tmpfs block and inode usage as ramfs inodes are created, removed, grown, and shrunk, then compute free counts with `max_blocks.saturating_sub(used_blocks)` and `max_inodes.saturating_sub(used_inodes)`. If tmpfs limits/accounting are not implemented yet, do not advertise free capacity that never decreases.

## Documentation

### `kernel/src/fs/fs_impls/tmpfs/fs.rs` line 55

> ```diff
> +        sb.magic = TMPFS_MAGIC;
> +        sb.blocks = max_blocks;
> +        sb.bfree = max_blocks;
> +        sb.bavail = max_blocks;
> +        sb.files = max_inodes;
> +        sb.ffree = max_inodes;
> ```

`linux-compat-docs` (minor): This changes user-visible `statfs`/`fstatfs` results for `tmpfs` by reporting `TMPFS_MAGIC` and capacity fields, but the Linux compatibility syscall coverage docs were not updated. The current coverage still only lists `statfs(path, buf);` and `fstatfs(fd, buf);`, so users cannot tell that `tmpfs`-specific `statfs` behavior is now supported.

**Fix.** Update `book/src/kernel/linux-compatibility/syscall-flag-coverage/file-systems-and-mount-control/README.md` and the matching `.scml` coverage file to document the newly supported `tmpfs` `statfs`/`fstatfs` return behavior.

## Retracted by verification

- `kernel/src/fs/fs_impls/tmpfs/fs.rs` line 3: retracted `qualified-fn-imports` because the cited rule covers free functions/statics imported through parent modules, while `TMPFS_MAGIC` is a `const`; the repository also has existing direct imports of filesystem magic constants, so this nit is not confirmed.
